# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/amount_parser.py
"""
amount_parser.py — Universal amount/currency parser.

Converts human-written amount strings from any locale to a Python float.
Standalone — no f_lightning, no network, no external dependencies.

Supported multiplier families:
  Western:     k/K, m/M, b/B, t/T, thousand, million, billion, trillion
  South Asian: lakh/lac/lacs, crore/crores
  CJK Chinese: 千 万 億/亿
  CJK Korean:  천 만 억
  Japanese:    千 万 億
  Currencies:  $  €  £  ¥  ₹  ₩  ₺  ฿  ₫  ₪  ₦  ₲  ₱  ₡  strips silently
  European:    "1.000,50"  →  1000.50  (before parsing)
"""

import re

# ── currency symbol strip ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
_CURRENCY_RE = re.compile(r"[$€£¥₹₩₺฿₫₪₦₲₱₡]")

# ── European decimal normaliser: 1.234,56  →  1234.56
#    Heuristic: if the last separator is a comma and a prior separator is a dot
#    (or the number has a dot-then-three-digits pattern) treat dot as thousands sep.
_EUR_DECIMAL_RE = re.compile(
    r"(\d{1,3})(?:\.(\d{3}))+,(\d+)"  # 1.234.567,89
)
_EUR_SIMPLE_RE = re.compile(
    r"(\d+),(\d{2})$"                  # 1000,50 at end of token
)

# ── multiplier table (lower-case key → float multiplier) ─────────────────────
_MULTIPLIERS: dict[str, float] = {
    # Western
    "thousand":  1_000,
    "k":         1_000,
    "million":   1_000_000,
    "m":         1_000_000,
    "billion":   1_000_000_000,
    "b":         1_000_000_000,
    "trillion":  1_000_000_000_000,
    "t":         1_000_000_000_000,
    # South Asian
    "lakh":      100_000,
    "lac":       100_000,
    "lacs":      100_000,
    "lakhs":     100_000,
    "crore":     10_000_000,
    "crores":    10_000_000,
    "cr":        10_000_000,
    # CJK Chinese / Japanese
    "千":        1_000,
    "万":        10_000,
    "亿":        100_000_000,
    "億":        100_000_000,
    # Korean
    "천":        1_000,
    "만":        10_000,
    "억":        100_000_000,
}

# Main pattern: optional sign, number (int or decimal), optional multiplier word
_AMOUNT_RE = re.compile(
    r"([+-]?\s*\d[\d,\.]*)"                   # numeric part (may contain , or .)
    r"\s*"
    r"(thousand|million|billion|trillion"
    r"|lakh[s]?|lac[s]?|crore[s]?|cr"
    r"|[kmbt]"                                 # single-letter (case-insensitive via flag)
    r"|千|万|亿|億|천|만|억"
    r")?",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)


def _normalize_decimal(text: str) -> str:
    """
    Convert European-style decimal notation to standard float notation.
    "1.234.567,89" → "1234567.89"
    "1.000,50"     → "1000.50"
    "1000,50"      → "1000.50"   (only if ends with comma + 2 digits)
    """
    # Full European: 1.234.567,89
    def _replace_eur(m):
        integer_part = m.group(0).replace(".", "").replace(",", ".")
        return integer_part

    result = _EUR_DECIMAL_RE.sub(_replace_eur, text)
    if result != text:
        return result

    # Simple: trailing ,DD
    result = _EUR_SIMPLE_RE.sub(lambda m: f"{m.group(1)}.{m.group(2)}", text)
    return result


def parse_amount(text: str) -> float | None:
    """
    Parse a human-written amount from any locale and return a float, or None.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> parse_amount("above 5 lakh")     # 500000.0
    >>> parse_amount("below 2 crore")    # 20000000.0
    >>> parse_amount("over 10k")         # 10000.0
    >>> parse_amount("above €1.000,50")  # 1000.5
    >>> parse_amount("5억")              # 500000000.0
    >>> parse_amount("random text")      # None
    """
    if not text:
        return None

    # Strip currency symbols
    cleaned = _CURRENCY_RE.sub("", text).strip()

    # Normalise European decimal separators
    cleaned = _normalize_decimal(cleaned)

    best: float | None = None

    for m in _AMOUNT_RE.finditer(cleaned):
        num_str = m.group(1)
        suffix  = (m.group(2) or "").strip().lower()

        # Remove thousands separators that are plain commas (Western style)
        # after European normalisation has already run
        num_str = num_str.replace(",", "").replace(" ", "")

        try:
            value = float(num_str)
        except ValueError:
            continue

        if suffix:
            mult = _MULTIPLIERS.get(suffix) or _MULTIPLIERS.get(suffix.rstrip("s"))
            if mult:
                value *= mult

        # Keep the largest valid match (handles "above 5 lakh" → ignore "5" alone)
        if best is None or abs(value) > abs(best):
            best = value

    return best


def extract_comparison_amount(text: str) -> tuple[str | None, float | None]:
    """
    Return (operator, amount) from a filter phrase.

    >>> extract_comparison_amount("above 5 lakh")   # (">=", 500000.0)
    >>> extract_comparison_amount("below 2 crore")  # ("<=", 20000000.0)
    >>> extract_comparison_amount("over 10k")       # (">",  10000.0)
    >>> extract_comparison_amount("less than 500")  # ("<",  500.0)
    """
    _OP_MAP = [
        (re.compile(r"\b(greater\s+than\s+or\s+equal|at\s+least|minimum|min)\b", re.I), ">="),
        (re.compile(r"\b(less\s+than\s+or\s+equal|at\s+most|maximum|max|up\s+to)\b", re.I), "<="),
        (re.compile(r"\b(greater\s+than|more\s+than|above|over|exceeds?|higher\s+than)\b", re.I), ">"),
        (re.compile(r"\b(less\s+than|below|under|lower\s+than)\b", re.I), "<"),
        (re.compile(r"\b(equal\s+to|equals?|exactly|=)\b", re.I), "="),
    ]

    op = None
    for pat, symbol in _OP_MAP:
        if pat.search(text):
            op = symbol
            break

    amount = parse_amount(text)
    return op, amount


def parse_amount_lightning(token: str) -> float | None:
    """
    Parse an amount token preferring the Go ParseAmount from f_lightning when
    Neural + Lightning provider is active.  Falls back to parse_amount() on any
    failure so the pure-Python path is always the air-gapped fallback.

    Same output contract as parse_amount() — returns a float or None.
    """
    try:
        import frappe
        settings = frappe.get_cached_doc("GenBI Settings")
        if getattr(settings, "llm_provider", "") == "Neural + Lightning":
            from f_genbi.f_genbi.genbi.lightning_client import lightning_parse_amount
            result = lightning_parse_amount(token)
            if result is not None:
                return float(result)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return parse_amount(token)

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/base_model_builder.py
"""
base_model_builder.py — Build and deploy a rule-distilled base slot-filler ONNX model
and a seed FAISS index from the static ERPNext QA corpus shipped with the app.

This runs once (via patch deploy_base_models.py) and writes to:
  sites/{site}/neural_models/slot_filler_base.onnx
  sites/{site}/neural_models/erpnext_qa.faiss
  sites/{site}/neural_models/erpnext_qa_meta.json

The "slot filler" produced here is a scikit-learn SGDClassifier distilled from
the same rule labels that slot_label_generator.py produces — no GPU required,
trains in < 5 seconds on the 800-example corpus.

If the full onnxruntime / faiss stack is not installed it writes a plain JSON
fallback that SchemaParser can use as a lookup table.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os

import frappe

# ── Static ERPNext QA corpus (shipped with the app) ───────────────────────────
# 800+ question → SQL pairs covering the most common ERPNext BI queries.
# Each entry: {"question": str, "sql": str, "intent": str, "entity": str}

_CORPUS_PATH = os.path.join(
    os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "..", "models", "erpnext_qa_corpus.json"
)

# ── Minimum corpus embedded in source (used when corpus file not present) ─────
_SEED_CORPUS = [
    {"question": "top 10 customers by revenue", "intent": "TOP_N", "entity": "Sales Invoice",
     "sql": "SELECT customer_name, SUM(grand_total) AS total FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 GROUP BY customer_name ORDER BY total DESC LIMIT 10"},
    {"question": "total sales this month", "intent": "AGGREGATE", "entity": "Sales Invoice",
     "sql": "SELECT SUM(grand_total) AS total FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 AND posting_date BETWEEN DATE_FORMAT(NOW(),'%Y-%m-01') AND LAST_DAY(NOW())"},
    {"question": "monthly revenue trend", "intent": "TREND", "entity": "Sales Invoice",
     "sql": "SELECT DATE_FORMAT(posting_date,'%Y-%m') AS month, SUM(grand_total) AS total FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 GROUP BY month ORDER BY month DESC LIMIT 24"},
    {"question": "outstanding invoices", "intent": "LIST", "entity": "Sales Invoice",
     "sql": "SELECT name, customer_name, grand_total, outstanding_amount FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 AND outstanding_amount>0 ORDER BY outstanding_amount DESC LIMIT 50"},
    {"question": "employee count by department", "intent": "COUNT", "entity": "Employee",
     "sql": "SELECT department, COUNT(name) AS count FROM `tabEmployee` WHERE status='Active' GROUP BY department ORDER BY count DESC"},
    {"question": "top 10 items by sales", "intent": "TOP_N", "entity": "Sales Invoice Item",
     "sql": "SELECT item_name, SUM(amount) AS total FROM `tabSales Invoice Item` JOIN `tabSales Invoice` si ON si.name=parent WHERE si.docstatus=1 GROUP BY item_name ORDER BY total DESC LIMIT 10"},
    {"question": "accounts receivable summary", "intent": "AGGREGATE", "entity": "Sales Invoice",
     "sql": "SELECT customer_name, SUM(outstanding_amount) AS outstanding FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 AND outstanding_amount>0 GROUP BY customer_name ORDER BY outstanding DESC LIMIT 50"},
    {"question": "purchase trend last 12 months", "intent": "TREND", "entity": "Purchase Invoice",
     "sql": "SELECT DATE_FORMAT(posting_date,'%Y-%m') AS month, SUM(grand_total) AS total FROM `tabPurchase Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 GROUP BY month ORDER BY month DESC LIMIT 12"},
    {"question": "pending sales orders", "intent": "LIST", "entity": "Sales Order",
     "sql": "SELECT name, customer_name, grand_total, status FROM `tabSales Order` WHERE docstatus=1 AND status NOT IN ('Closed','Completed','Cancelled') ORDER BY delivery_date ASC LIMIT 50"},
    {"question": "low stock items", "intent": "LIST", "entity": "Bin",
     "sql": "SELECT item_code, warehouse, actual_qty, reorder_level FROM `tabBin` b JOIN `tabItem` i ON i.name=b.item_code WHERE b.actual_qty<=i.reorder_level AND i.disabled=0 ORDER BY actual_qty ASC LIMIT 50"},
]


def _model_dir() -> str:
    return os.path.join(frappe.get_site_path(), "neural_models")


def _ensure_dir():
    os.makedirs(_model_dir(), exist_ok=True)


def _load_corpus() -> list[dict]:
    if os.path.isfile(_CORPUS_PATH):
        try:
            with open(_CORPUS_PATH) as f:
                corpus = json.load(f)
            if corpus:
                return corpus
        except Exception:
            pass
    return _SEED_CORPUS


def deploy_base_models(force: bool = False) -> dict:
    """
    Build and deploy base models to sites/{site}/neural_models/.
    Skips if models already exist (unless force=True).

    Returns {"slot_filler": bool, "faiss": bool, "fallback_json": bool}
    """
    _ensure_dir()
    corpus = _load_corpus()

    results = {
        "slot_filler": False,
        "faiss": False,
        "fallback_json": False,
        "corpus_size": len(corpus),
    }

    # ── 1. Try full ONNX slot filler build ───────────────────────────────────
    sf_path = os.path.join(_model_dir(), "slot_filler_base.onnx")
    if not os.path.isfile(sf_path) or force:
        results["slot_filler"] = _build_slot_filler_onnx(corpus, sf_path)

    # Copy to slot_filler.onnx (the canonical name model_registry uses)
    # only if the canonical one doesn't already exist (protect user-trained models)
    canonical = os.path.join(_model_dir(), "slot_filler.onnx")
    if not os.path.isfile(canonical) and os.path.isfile(sf_path):
        import shutil
        shutil.copy2(sf_path, canonical)

    # ── 2. Try FAISS index build ─────────────────────────────────────────────
    faiss_path = os.path.join(_model_dir(), "erpnext_qa.faiss")
    meta_path  = os.path.join(_model_dir(), "erpnext_qa_meta.json")
    if not os.path.isfile(faiss_path) or force:
        results["faiss"] = _build_faiss_index(corpus, faiss_path, meta_path)

    # ── 3. Fallback JSON lookup (always written — fast lookup without FAISS) ──
    fallback_path = os.path.join(_model_dir(), "erpnext_qa_fallback.json")
    if not os.path.isfile(fallback_path) or force:
        try:
            _write_fallback_json(corpus, fallback_path)
            results["fallback_json"] = True
        except Exception as e:
            frappe.logger().warning(f"base_model_builder: fallback JSON write failed: {e}")

    frappe.logger().info(f"base_model_builder: deploy complete — {results}")
    return results


# ── Slot filler build ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _build_slot_filler_onnx(corpus: list[dict], out_path: str) -> bool:
    """
    Train a tiny SGDClassifier on TF-IDF features from the corpus and
    export to ONNX.  ~800 examples, trains in < 3 seconds.
    """
    try:
        from sklearn.pipeline import Pipeline
        from sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer
        from sklearn.linear_model import SGDClassifier
        from skl2onnx import convert_sklearn
        from skl2onnx.common.data_types import StringTensorType
    except ImportError:
        frappe.logger().info("base_model_builder: sklearn/skl2onnx not installed — skipping ONNX build")
        return False

    texts  = [e["question"] for e in corpus]
    labels = [e["intent"]   for e in corpus]

    pipe = Pipeline([
        ("tfidf", TfidfVectorizer(ngram_range=(1, 2), max_features=2000)),
        ("clf",   SGDClassifier(loss="modified_huber", max_iter=200, random_state=42)),
    ])
    pipe.fit(texts, labels)

    try:
        initial_type = [("string_input", StringTensorType([None, 1]))]
        onnx_model = convert_sklearn(pipe, initial_types=initial_type,
                                     target_opset=15)
        with open(out_path, "wb") as f:
            f.write(onnx_model.SerializeToString())
        frappe.logger().info(f"base_model_builder: slot_filler_base.onnx written ({len(corpus)} examples)")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        frappe.logger().warning(f"base_model_builder: ONNX export failed: {e}")
        return False


# ── FAISS index build ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _build_faiss_index(corpus: list[dict], faiss_path: str, meta_path: str) -> bool:
    """
    Build a FAISS flat-L2 index from sentence-transformers embeddings.
    Falls back gracefully if faiss-cpu or sentence-transformers are absent.
    """
    try:
        import faiss
        from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
        import numpy as np
    except ImportError:
        frappe.logger().info("base_model_builder: faiss/sentence-transformers not installed — skipping FAISS build")
        return False

    try:
        model = SentenceTransformer("all-MiniLM-L6-v2")
        texts = [e["question"] for e in corpus]
        embeddings = model.encode(texts, convert_to_numpy=True,
                                  show_progress_bar=False).astype("float32")
        faiss.normalize_L2(embeddings)

        index = faiss.IndexFlatIP(embeddings.shape[1])
        index.add(embeddings)
        faiss.write_index(index, faiss_path)

        meta = [{"question": e["question"], "sql": e.get("sql", ""),
                 "intent": e.get("intent", ""), "entity": e.get("entity", "")}
                for e in corpus]
        with open(meta_path, "w") as f:
            json.dump(meta, f, indent=2)

        frappe.logger().info(f"base_model_builder: erpnext_qa.faiss written ({len(corpus)} vectors)")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        frappe.logger().warning(f"base_model_builder: FAISS build failed: {e}")
        return False


# ── Fallback JSON lookup ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _write_fallback_json(corpus: list[dict], out_path: str):
    """
    Write a simple JSON lookup keyed by lowercased question fragments.
    Used by SchemaParser when FAISS is not available — fast dict lookup.
    """
    lookup = {}
    for e in corpus:
        key = e["question"].lower().strip()
        lookup[key] = {
            "sql": e.get("sql", ""),
            "intent": e.get("intent", ""),
            "entity": e.get("entity", ""),
        }
    with open(out_path, "w") as f:
        json.dump(lookup, f, indent=2)

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/data_dictionary.py
"""
Task 4.5 — GenBI Data Dictionary

Auto-generates rich schema documentation for ERPNext DocTypes, including:
  - Field list with types, options, descriptions
  - Link/relationship map (outgoing + incoming links)
  - Row count and submittability
  - Cached in Redis for 1 hour

Entry points:
  get_doctype_docs(doctype)        → {fields, relationships, stats, summary}
  get_data_dictionary(module=None) → [{doctype, fields_count, row_count, ...}, ...]
  search_dictionary(term)          → [{doctype, field, label, description}, ...]
"""

import frappe
import json


_CACHE_KEY_PREFIX = "genbi_dict_v1_"
_CACHE_TTL = 3600          # 1 hour
_INDEX_CACHE_KEY = "genbi_dict_index_v1"


# ── Public helpers ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def get_doctype_docs(doctype: str) -> dict:
    """Return full documentation for a single DocType.

    Cached in Redis for 1 h.
    """
    cache_key = _CACHE_KEY_PREFIX + frappe.utils.scrub(doctype)
    cached = frappe.cache().get_value(cache_key)
    if cached:
        try:
            return json.loads(cached) if isinstance(cached, str) else cached
        except Exception:
            pass

    doc = _build_doctype_docs(doctype)
    try:
        frappe.cache().set_value(cache_key, json.dumps(doc, default=str), expires_in_sec=_CACHE_TTL)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return doc


def get_data_dictionary(module: str = None, limit: int = 100) -> list:
    """Return a summary list of all documented DocTypes.

    Optionally filter by Frappe module. Cached for 1 h.
    """
    cached = frappe.cache().get_value(_INDEX_CACHE_KEY)
    if cached:
        try:
            index = json.loads(cached) if isinstance(cached, str) else cached
        except Exception:
            index = None
    else:
        index = None

    if index is None:
        index = _build_index()
        try:
            frappe.cache().set_value(_INDEX_CACHE_KEY, json.dumps(index, default=str), expires_in_sec=_CACHE_TTL)
        except Exception:
            pass

    if module:
        index = [i for i in index if i.get("module", "").lower() == module.lower()]

    return index[:limit]


def search_dictionary(term: str) -> list:
    """Full-text search across DocType names, field names, labels, and descriptions."""
    term = (term or "").strip().lower()
    if not term or len(term) < 2:
        return []

    rows = frappe.db.sql("""
        SELECT
            df.parent AS doctype,
            df.fieldname AS field,
            df.label,
            df.fieldtype,
            COALESCE(df.description, '') AS description
        FROM `tabDocField` df
        WHERE (
            LOWER(df.parent) LIKE %(t)s
            OR LOWER(df.fieldname) LIKE %(t)s
            OR LOWER(df.label) LIKE %(t)s
            OR LOWER(COALESCE(df.description, '')) LIKE %(t)s
        )
        AND df.parent NOT LIKE '%%Log%%'
        AND df.parent NOT LIKE '%%Version%%'
        ORDER BY df.parent, df.idx
        LIMIT 80
    """, {"t": f"%{term}%"}, as_dict=True)

    return [dict(r) for r in rows]


def invalidate_cache(doctype: str = None):
    """Invalidate dictionary cache for a single DocType, or the whole index."""
    if doctype:
        frappe.cache().delete_value(_CACHE_KEY_PREFIX + frappe.utils.scrub(doctype))
    else:
        frappe.cache().delete_value(_INDEX_CACHE_KEY)


# ── Builders ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _build_doctype_docs(doctype: str) -> dict:
    try:
        meta = frappe.get_meta(doctype)
    except Exception as e:
        return {"success": False, "error": str(e)}

    # Fields
    field_docs = []
    for f in meta.fields:
        if f.fieldtype in ("Column Break", "Section Break", "Tab Break", "Fold",
                           "Heading", "HTML", "Custom HTML"):
            continue
        field_docs.append({
            "fieldname": f.fieldname,
            "label": f.label or f.fieldname,
            "fieldtype": f.fieldtype,
            "options": f.options or "",
            "reqd": bool(getattr(f, "reqd", 0)),
            "in_list_view": bool(getattr(f, "in_list_view", 0)),
            "bold": bool(getattr(f, "bold", 0)),
            "description": f.description or "",
        })

    # Outgoing links (this DocType → other DocTypes)
    outgoing = [
        {"fieldname": f.fieldname, "label": f.label, "links_to": f.options}
        for f in meta.fields if f.fieldtype == "Link" and f.options
    ]

    # Incoming links (other DocTypes → this DocType)
    incoming_rows = frappe.db.sql("""
        SELECT parent AS from_doctype, fieldname, label
        FROM `tabDocField`
        WHERE fieldtype = 'Link' AND options = %s
        AND parent NOT LIKE '%%Log%%'
        LIMIT 30
    """, (doctype,), as_dict=True)

    incoming = [{"from_doctype": r.from_doctype, "fieldname": r.fieldname, "label": r.label}
                for r in incoming_rows]

    # Stats
    try:
        row_count = frappe.db.count(doctype)
    except Exception:
        row_count = None

    return {
        "success": True,
        "doctype": doctype,
        "module": meta.module,
        "is_submittable": bool(meta.is_submittable),
        "is_single": bool(meta.issingle),
        "is_child": bool(meta.istable),
        "field_count": len(field_docs),
        "fields": field_docs,
        "outgoing_links": outgoing,
        "incoming_links": incoming,
        "row_count": row_count,
        "table_name": f"tab{doctype}",
    }


def _build_index() -> list:
    """Build the lightweight dictionary index — one entry per DocType."""
    doctypes = frappe.get_all(
        "DocType",
        filters={"istable": 0, "issingle": 0},
        fields=["name", "module", "is_submittable", "description"],
        order_by="module, name",
        limit=300,
        ignore_permissions=True,
    )

    index = []
    for dt in doctypes:
        try:
            row_count = frappe.db.count(dt.name)
        except Exception:
            row_count = None

        try:
            field_count = frappe.db.count("DocField", {"parent": dt.name})
        except Exception:
            field_count = 0

        index.append({
            "doctype": dt.name,
            "module": dt.module,
            "is_submittable": bool(dt.is_submittable),
            "description": dt.description or "",
            "field_count": field_count,
            "row_count": row_count,
            "table_name": f"tab{dt.name}",
        })

    return index

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/duckdb_engine.py
"""
duckdb_engine.py — In-process DuckDB analytics for queries that MySQL can't handle:
  window functions, PIVOT, running totals, cumulative sums, percentile rank.

Architecture:
  1. Fetch the required rows from MySQL (docstatus-filtered, permission-checked).
  2. Load into an in-process DuckDB instance (no server, no disk, pure in-memory).
  3. Run the analytic SQL against DuckDB.
  4. Return rows in the same format as _execute_query().

DuckDB is imported lazily — if not installed the engine raises ImportError and
NeuralEngine falls back to the standard MySQL path.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re

import frappe


# ── Window / analytic keyword detector ────────────────────────────────────────

_WINDOW_KEYWORDS = re.compile(
    r"\b(running\s+total|running\s+sum|cumulative|rank(ed)?|percentile|pivot"
    r"|window\s+function|over\s+time\s+cumulative|nth\s+row|row_number"
    r"|dense_rank|ntile|lag\b|lead\b|first_value|last_value)\b",
    re.I,
)


def is_duckdb_query(question: str) -> bool:
    """Return True when question requires window/analytic SQL beyond MySQL's reach."""
    return bool(_WINDOW_KEYWORDS.search(question))


# ── DuckDB query templates ────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _build_duckdb_sql(question: str, doctype: str, rows: list[dict]) -> str | None:
    """
    Map question intent to a DuckDB SQL string that operates on a table
    called `src` (the rows fetched from MySQL).

    Returns None if no template matches — caller falls back to MySQL result.
    """
    q = question.lower()

    # Running total / cumulative sum
    if re.search(r"\brunning\s+total\b|\bcumulative\b|\brunning\s+sum\b", q):
        val_col = _pick_value_col(rows)
        grp_col = _pick_group_col(rows)
        if val_col and grp_col:
            return (
                f"SELECT *, SUM({val_col}) OVER (ORDER BY {grp_col} "
                f"ROWS BETWEEN UNBOUNDED PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW) AS running_total "
                f"FROM src ORDER BY {grp_col}"
            )

    # Rank by value
    if re.search(r"\brank(ed)?\b|\bdense_rank\b", q):
        val_col = _pick_value_col(rows)
        if val_col:
            return (
                f"SELECT *, RANK() OVER (ORDER BY {val_col} DESC) AS rank "
                f"FROM src ORDER BY rank"
            )

    # Percentile
    if re.search(r"\bpercentile\b|\bntile\b", q):
        val_col = _pick_value_col(rows)
        n_match = re.search(r"\b(\d+)\s+(?:bucket|tile|group)", q)
        n = int(n_match.group(1)) if n_match else 4
        if val_col:
            return (
                f"SELECT *, NTILE({n}) OVER (ORDER BY {val_col} DESC) AS bucket "
                f"FROM src ORDER BY {val_col} DESC"
            )

    # Pivot (months as columns — common pattern)
    if re.search(r"\bpivot\b", q):
        return _build_pivot_sql(rows)

    # Row number
    if re.search(r"\brow_number\b|\bnth\s+row\b", q):
        return "SELECT ROW_NUMBER() OVER () AS row_num, * FROM src"

    # Lag / lead (period-over-period comparison)
    if re.search(r"\blag\b|\blead\b|\bperiod.over.period\b|\bmonth.over.month\b", q):
        val_col = _pick_value_col(rows)
        per_col = _pick_period_col(rows)
        if val_col and per_col:
            return (
                f"SELECT *, LAG({val_col}) OVER (ORDER BY {per_col}) AS prev_period_value, "
                f"ROUND(({val_col} - LAG({val_col}) OVER (ORDER BY {per_col})) * 100.0 / "
                f"NULLIF(LAG({val_col}) OVER (ORDER BY {per_col}), 0), 2) AS pct_change "
                f"FROM src ORDER BY {per_col}"
            )

    return None


def _build_pivot_sql(rows: list[dict]) -> str | None:
    """Build a simple PIVOT SQL for monthly data."""
    if not rows:
        return None
    per_col = _pick_period_col(rows)
    val_col = _pick_value_col(rows)
    grp_col = _pick_group_col(rows, exclude={per_col, val_col})
    if not (per_col and val_col and grp_col):
        return None
    # DuckDB PIVOT syntax
    return (
        f"PIVOT src ON {per_col} USING SUM({val_col}) GROUP BY {grp_col}"
    )


# ── Core execution ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def execute_duckdb(question: str, doctype: str, mysql_sql: str) -> tuple[list, list] | None:
    """
    Fetch rows from MySQL via mysql_sql, then run DuckDB analytics on them.

    Returns (rows, columns) in the same format as _execute_query(), or None
    if DuckDB is not installed or no template matches.
    """
    try:
        import duckdb
    except ImportError:
        frappe.logger().debug("duckdb_engine: duckdb not installed — skipping")
        return None

    # Fetch base data from MySQL
    try:
        mysql_rows = frappe.db.sql(mysql_sql, as_dict=True)
    except Exception as e:
        frappe.logger().debug(f"duckdb_engine: MySQL fetch failed: {e}")
        return None

    if not mysql_rows:
        return [], []

    # Build DuckDB analytic SQL
    analytic_sql = _build_duckdb_sql(question, doctype, mysql_rows)
    if not analytic_sql:
        return None  # no DuckDB template matched — caller uses MySQL result

    try:
        con = duckdb.connect(":memory:")
        import pandas as pd
        df = pd.DataFrame(mysql_rows)
        con.register("src", df)
        result_df = con.execute(analytic_sql).df()
        rows = result_df.to_dict(orient="records")
        cols = list(result_df.columns)
        con.close()
        return rows, cols
    except Exception as e:
        frappe.logger().debug(f"duckdb_engine: DuckDB query failed: {e}")
        return None


# ── MySQL base SQL for common DuckDB-routed queries ───────────────────────────

def mysql_base_sql_for(question: str, doctype: str) -> str:
    """
    Return a MySQL SELECT to fetch the raw data rows that DuckDB will aggregate.
    Keeps it simple — fetches all columns needed for the analytic query.
    """
    q = question.lower()
    table = f"tab{doctype}"

    if "sales" in q or "invoice" in q or "revenue" in q:
        return (
            f"SELECT DATE_FORMAT(posting_date,'%Y-%m') AS period, "
            f"customer_name, SUM(grand_total) AS total_revenue "
            f"FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 "
            f"GROUP BY period, customer_name ORDER BY period"
        )
    if "purchase" in q:
        return (
            f"SELECT DATE_FORMAT(posting_date,'%Y-%m') AS period, "
            f"supplier_name, SUM(grand_total) AS total_purchase "
            f"FROM `tabPurchase Invoice` WHERE docstatus=1 "
            f"GROUP BY period, supplier_name ORDER BY period"
        )
    # Generic fallback
    return f"SELECT * FROM `{table}` WHERE docstatus=1 LIMIT 1000"


# ── Column helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _pick_value_col(rows: list[dict]) -> str | None:
    if not rows:
        return None
    preferred = ("total_revenue", "total_purchase", "total", "grand_total",
                 "net_total", "amount", "value", "qty")
    for k in preferred:
        if k in rows[0]:
            return k
    for k, v in rows[0].items():
        if isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool):
            return k
    return None


def _pick_group_col(rows: list[dict], exclude: set | None = None) -> str | None:
    if not rows:
        return None
    exclude = exclude or set()
    preferred = ("customer_name", "supplier_name", "item_name", "employee_name",
                 "department", "territory", "period", "month")
    for k in preferred:
        if k in rows[0] and k not in exclude:
            return k
    # First string-valued column
    for k, v in rows[0].items():
        if k not in exclude and isinstance(v, str):
            return k
    return None


def _pick_period_col(rows: list[dict]) -> str | None:
    if not rows:
        return None
    for k in ("period", "month", "week", "year", "quarter", "posting_date", "date"):
        if k in rows[0]:
            return k
    return None

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/explainer_i18n.py

    "trend_flat_label": "مستقر",
    "count_result":   "تم العثور على {count:,} {entity}{period}.",
    "count_none":     "لم يُعثر على {entity}{period}.",
    "list_result":    "يعرض {n} {entity}{period}{hint}.",
    "list_hint":      " ({n} من {limit}+)",
    "list_none":      "لم يُعثر على {entity}{period}.",
    "compare_header": "مقارنة {entity}:",
    "compare_row":    "  {period}: {value}",
    "compare_change": "  {arrow} تغيير {pct:+.1f}%",
    "lookup_found1":  "تم العثور على: {name} ({entity})",
    "lookup_found_n": "تم العثور على {n} {entity} مطابق لبحثك.",
    "lookup_none":    "لم يُعثر على {entity}{hint}.",
    "lookup_hint":    " مطابق لـ '{term}'",
    "generic_found":  "تم العثور على {n} {entity}.",
    "generic_none":   "لم يُعثر على {entity}.",
    "fn_sum":   "إجمالي",
    "fn_avg":   "متوسط",
    "fn_max":   "أقصى",
    "fn_min":   "أدنى",
    "fn_count": "عدد",
}

_PACKS: dict[str, dict] = {
    "en": _EN,
    "hi": _HI,
    "ar": _AR,
}


def current_locale() -> str:
    """Return the active Frappe locale code, normalised to 2 chars."""
    try:
        import frappe
        lang = getattr(frappe.local, "lang", None) or "en"
        return lang[:2].lower()
    except Exception:
        return "en"


def get_templates(locale: str | None = None) -> dict:
    """
    Return the template pack for the given locale (or the active Frappe locale).
    Falls back to English for any missing keys.
    """
    loc = (locale or current_locale())[:2].lower()
    pack = _PACKS.get(loc, {})
    if pack is _EN:
        return pack
    # Merge with English so missing keys always resolve
    merged = dict(_EN)
    merged.update(pack)
    return merged


def is_rtl(locale: str | None = None) -> bool:
    """Return True for right-to-left locales."""
    loc = (locale or current_locale())[:2].lower()
    return loc in ("ar", "he", "fa", "ur")

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/forecaster.py
"""
Task 4.1 — GenBI Predictive Analytics

Provides time-series trend forecasting using scikit-learn linear regression
and simple exponential smoothing for short-term projections.

Entry point: forecast(question) — returns {success, explanation, data, chart_config}

Supported intents (matched in process_question before LLM):
  "forecast sales next 3 months"
  "predict revenue for next quarter"
  "what will purchases be next month"
  "project stock levels next 6 weeks"
  "trend forecast for outstanding amount"

Uses real data from the DB, fits a linear trend, then projects N periods forward.
"""

import re
import frappe
from frappe import _

# ── Intent pattern ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

FORECAST_PAT = re.compile(
    r"\b(forecast|predict|projection|project|what\s+will|future|next\s+\d+\s*(month|week|quarter|year))\b",
    re.I,
)

# ── Metric registry ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Maps keyword → (label, sql_template, period_field, value_field, period_unit)
_METRIC_MAP = {
    "sales": (
        "Sales Revenue",
        """SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`, '%Y-%m') AS period,
                  SUM(`grand_total`) AS value
           FROM `tabSales Invoice`
           WHERE `docstatus` = 1
             AND `posting_date` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL {lookback} MONTH)
           GROUP BY period
           ORDER BY period""",
        "month",
    ),
    "revenue": (
        "Sales Revenue",
        """SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`, '%Y-%m') AS period,
                  SUM(`grand_total`) AS value
           FROM `tabSales Invoice`
           WHERE `docstatus` = 1
             AND `posting_date` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL {lookback} MONTH)
           GROUP BY period
           ORDER BY period""",
        "month",
    ),
    "purchase": (
        "Purchases",
        """SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`, '%Y-%m') AS period,
                  SUM(`grand_total`) AS value
           FROM `tabPurchase Invoice`
           WHERE `docstatus` = 1
             AND `posting_date` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL {lookback} MONTH)
           GROUP BY period
           ORDER BY period""",
        "month",
    ),
    "outstanding": (
        "Outstanding Receivables",
        """SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`, '%Y-%m') AS period,
                  SUM(`outstanding_amount`) AS value
           FROM `tabSales Invoice`
           WHERE `docstatus` = 1
             AND `outstanding_amount` > 0
             AND `posting_date` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL {lookback} MONTH)
           GROUP BY period
           ORDER BY period""",
        "month",
    ),
    "expense": (
        "Expenses",
        """SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`, '%Y-%m') AS period,
                  SUM(`grand_total`) AS value
           FROM `tabPurchase Invoice`
           WHERE `docstatus` = 1
             AND `posting_date` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL {lookback} MONTH)
           GROUP BY period
           ORDER BY period""",
        "month",
    ),
    "order": (
        "Sales Orders",
        """SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`transaction_date`, '%Y-%m') AS period,
                  SUM(`grand_total`) AS value
           FROM `tabSales Order`
           WHERE `docstatus` = 1
             AND `transaction_date` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL {lookback} MONTH)
           GROUP BY period
           ORDER BY period""",
        "month",
    ),
}


def forecast(question: str) -> dict:
    """Main entry point for Predictive Analytics.

    Returns {success, explanation, data, chart_config, forecast_periods,
             historical_periods, metric_label, trend_direction}
    """
    q = question.strip().lower()

    # Detect metric
    metric_key = None
    for key in _METRIC_MAP:
        if key in q:
            metric_key = key
            break
    if not metric_key:
        metric_key = "sales"  # Default to sales

    # Detect number of forecast periods
    n_match = re.search(r"next\s+(\d+)\s*(month|week|quarter|year)", q, re.I)
    n_periods = 3  # default
    period_unit = "month"
    if n_match:
        n_periods = int(n_match.group(1))
        unit = n_match.group(2).lower()
        if unit == "quarter":
            n_periods = n_periods * 3
            period_unit = "month"
        elif unit == "year":
            n_periods = n_periods * 12
            period_unit = "month"
        elif unit == "week":
            period_unit = "week"
        else:
            period_unit = "month"
        n_periods = min(n_periods, 12)  # cap at 12 forward periods

    label, sql_template, _ = _METRIC_MAP[metric_key]
    lookback = max(12, n_periods * 2)  # at least 12 months of history

    try:
        sql = sql_template.format(lookback=lookback)
        rows = frappe.db.sql(sql, as_dict=True)
    except Exception as e:
        return {"success": False, "error": str(e)}

    if len(rows) < 3:
        return {
            "success": True,
            "explanation": f"Not enough historical data to forecast {label}. "
                           f"At least 3 periods of data are needed.",
            "data": [],
            "chart_config": None,
        }

    periods = [r["period"] for r in rows]
    values = [float(r.get("value") or 0) for r in rows]
    n = len(values)

    forecast_labels = _next_periods(periods[-1], n_periods, period_unit)

    # ── Holt-Winters (frappe_rs) when RPG Boost active; else LinearRegression ──
    y_pred = None
    trend_direction = "flat"
    slope = 0.0
    r2 = 0.0
    method = "linear"

    try:
        from f_genbi.f_genbi.genbi.rpg_bridge import rpg_holtwinters, is_rpg_available
        settings = frappe.get_cached_doc("GenBI Settings")
        if is_rpg_available() and getattr(settings, "llm_provider", "") == "Neural + RPG Boost":
            import json as _json
            data_json = _json.dumps([
                {"period": p, "value": v} for p, v in zip(periods, values)
            ])
            hw_json = rpg_holtwinters(data_json, n_periods)
            if hw_json:
                hw_result = _json.loads(hw_json)
                y_pred = [max(0.0, float(x.get("forecast", 0))) for x in hw_result]
                method = "holt-winters"
    except Exception as e:
        frappe.logger().debug(f"forecaster: Holt-Winters failed, falling back: {e}")

    if y_pred is None:
        # ── Fallback: scikit-learn LinearRegression ──────────────────────────
        try:
            from sklearn.linear_model import LinearRegression
            import numpy as np
        except ImportError:
            return {"success": False, "error": "scikit-learn or numpy not installed."}

        X = np.arange(n).reshape(-1, 1)
        y = np.array(values)
        model = LinearRegression()
        model.fit(X, y)
        r2 = float(model.score(X, y))
        slope = float(model.coef_[0])
        X_future = np.arange(n, n + n_periods).reshape(-1, 1)
        y_pred = [max(0.0, float(v)) for v in model.predict(X_future)]

    trend_direction = (
        "upward" if values[-1] < (y_pred[-1] if y_pred else values[-1])
        else "downward" if values[-1] > (y_pred[-1] if y_pred else values[-1])
        else "flat"
    )
    if slope:
        trend_direction = "upward" if slope > 0 else "downward" if slope < 0 else "flat"

    # ── Build combined dataset for chart ────────────────────────────────────
    chart_data = []
    for period, value in zip(periods, values):
        chart_data.append({"period": period, "actual": round(value, 2), "forecast": None})
    for period, pred in zip(forecast_labels, y_pred):
        chart_data.append({"period": period, "actual": None, "forecast": round(pred, 2)})

    # ── Build explanation ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    last_actual = values[-1]
    avg_forecast = sum(y_pred) / len(y_pred)
    change_pct = ((avg_forecast - last_actual) / last_actual * 100) if last_actual else 0

    model_note = " (Holt-Winters)" if method == "holt-winters" else f" (slope: {slope:+,.2f}/period, R²: {r2:.2f})"
    explanation = (
        f"**{label} Forecast — next {n_periods} {period_unit}(s)**\n\n"
        f"Based on {n} periods of historical data, the trend is **{trend_direction}**{model_note}.\n\n"
        f"Projected average {label.lower()} over the next {n_periods} period(s): "
        f"**{avg_forecast:,.2f}** ({change_pct:+.1f}% vs last period)."
    )

    if method != "holt-winters" and r2 < 0.4:
        explanation += (
            "\n\n⚠️ *Low R² score indicates high variability — treat this forecast as indicative only.*"
        )

    chart_config = {
        "type": "line",
        "data": {
            "labels": [r["period"] for r in chart_data],
            "datasets": [
                {"name": "Actual", "values": [r["actual"] for r in chart_data]},
                {"name": "Forecast", "values": [r["forecast"] for r in chart_data]},
            ],
        },
        "title": f"{label} Forecast",
        "colors": ["#5e64ff", "#ff5e5e"],
        "lineOptions": {"hideDots": 0, "regionFill": 0},
    }

    return {
        "success": True,
        "explanation": explanation,
        "data": chart_data,
        "chart_config": chart_config,
        "forecast_periods": forecast_labels,
        "historical_periods": n,
        "metric_label": label,
        "trend_direction": trend_direction,
        "r2_score": round(r2, 3),
        "slope": round(slope, 4),
        "forecast_method": method,
    }


def _next_periods(last_period: str, n: int, unit: str) -> list:
    """Generate the next N period labels after last_period.

    Supports monthly (YYYY-MM) and weekly (YYYY-WNN) formats.
    """
    import datetime

    labels = []
    try:
        if unit == "week":
            # last_period may be YYYY-WNN or YYYY-MM (fall back to month)
            try:
                year, week = int(last_period[:4]), int(last_period[6:]) if "W" in last_period else 1
            except (ValueError, IndexError):
                year = int(last_period[:4])
                week = 1
            for i in range(1, n + 1):
                w = week + i
                y = year + (w - 1) // 52
                w = ((w - 1) % 52) + 1
                labels.append(f"{y}-W{w:02d}")
        else:
            # Monthly: YYYY-MM
            year = int(last_period[:4])
            month = int(last_period[5:7])
            for i in range(1, n + 1):
                month += 1
                if month > 12:
                    month = 1
                    year += 1
                labels.append(f"{year}-{month:02d}")
    except Exception:
        for i in range(1, n + 1):
            labels.append(f"F+{i}")

    return labels

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/lightning_client.py
"""
lightning_client.py — Runtime guard and HTTP client for the f_lightning Go sidecar.

The sidecar exposes:
  GET  /search?q=<question>&doctype=<DocType>   → MeiliSearch NLP search
  GET  /suggest?q=<text>                         → autocomplete
  GET  /autocomplete?q=<text>                    → autocomplete

It also has an NLP BuildQuery pipeline that returns structured filters,
and ParseAmount for Indian currency strings (k / lakh / cr).

All public functions in this module return None / fallback values when
f_lightning is not installed or the sidecar is unreachable — callers never
need to guard against ConnectionError or ImportError.

Sidecar base URL is read from:
  frappe.conf.lightning_sidecar_url   (preferred)
  or http://localhost:8765             (default from f_lightning config.yaml)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json

import frappe

_DEFAULT_BASE = "http://localhost:8765"
_CONNECT_TIMEOUT = 2.0   # seconds — fast fail so GenBI stays responsive
_READ_TIMEOUT    = 5.0


def _sidecar_url() -> str:
    try:
        url = frappe.conf.get("lightning_sidecar_url", _DEFAULT_BASE)
        return url.rstrip("/")
    except Exception:
        return _DEFAULT_BASE


def _get_client():
    """
    Return a LightningClient if f_lightning is installed, else None.
    Import is attempted once; result is module-level cached.
    """
    return _client_singleton()


# ── Singleton with lazy import ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

_client_cache: "LightningClient | None | bool" = False  # False = not attempted


def _client_singleton() -> "LightningClient | None":
    global _client_cache
    if _client_cache is not False:
        return _client_cache  # type: ignore[return-value]
    try:
        import f_lightning  # noqa: F401 — just confirms it's installed
        _client_cache = LightningClient(_sidecar_url())
    except ImportError:
        _client_cache = None
    return _client_cache  # type: ignore[return-value]


def is_lightning_available() -> bool:
    return _get_client() is not None


# ── LightningClient ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

class LightningClient:
    """Thin HTTP wrapper around the f_lightning Go sidecar."""

    def __init__(self, base_url: str):
        self.base_url = base_url
        self._session = None

    def _session_get(self):
        """Lazily create a requests.Session for connection pooling."""
        if self._session is None:
            try:
                import requests
                s = requests.Session()
                # Inject Frappe site name header so the sidecar can resolve the tenant
                s.headers["X-Frappe-Site-Name"] = frappe.local.site
                self._session = s
            except ImportError:
                pass
        return self._session

    def _get(self, path: str, params: dict | None = None) -> dict | None:
        session = self._session_get()
        if not session:
            return None
        try:
            resp = session.get(
                f"{self.base_url}{path}",
                params=params or {},
                timeout=(_CONNECT_TIMEOUT, _READ_TIMEOUT),
            )
            resp.raise_for_status()
            return resp.json()
        except Exception as e:
            frappe.logger().debug(f"LightningClient._get {path}: {e}")
            return None

    # ── Public API ────────────────────��──────────────────────────────────��─────

    def ping_meilisearch(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if the sidecar is reachable (uses /health or first /search call)."""
        result = self._get("/search", {"q": "ping", "limit": "1"})
        return result is not None

    def ping_nlp(self) -> bool:
        """Return True if the NLP endpoint responds."""
        result = self._get("/search", {"q": "test"})
        return result is not None

    def nlp_search(self, question: str, doctype: str | None = None) -> dict | None:
        """
        Run a natural-language search through the sidecar.
        Returns the full response dict or None on failure.

        Response shape:
          {hits: [...], parsed: {DocType, Filters, Text, Limit}, took_ms: N,
           total: N, ai_enabled: bool, is_semantic: bool}
        """
        params: dict = {"q": question}
        if doctype:
            params["doctype"] = doctype
        return self._get("/search", params)

    def nlp_parse(self, question: str) -> dict | None:
        """
        Return the parsed NLP structure for question without running MeiliSearch.
        Uses the /search endpoint and extracts the `parsed` field.
        """
        result = self.nlp_search(question)
        if result:
            return result.get("parsed")
        return None

    def parse_amount(self, token: str) -> int | None:
        """
        Ask the sidecar to parse an Indian currency token via Go ParseAmount.
        Falls back to a Python implementation if the sidecar is unavailable.

        The sidecar exposes ParseAmount logic inside the NLP pipeline, so we
        replicate it locally here as a Python fallback (same logic as rules.go).
        """
        return _py_parse_amount(token)


# ── Module-level convenience functions ────────────────────────────────────────

def call_lightning_search(question: str, doctype: str | None = None) -> dict | None:
    """
    Route question through MeiliSearch via the f_lightning sidecar.
    Returns the full response dict (hits, parsed, took_ms, …) or None.
    """
    client = _get_client()
    if not client:
        return None
    return client.nlp_search(question, doctype)


def call_lightning_nlp_parse(question: str) -> dict | None:
    """
    Return the structured NLP parse of question from the Go pipeline.
    Useful for pre-populating NeuralSQLBuilder filters.
    Returns dict with keys: DocType, Filters, Text, Limit — or None.
    """
    client = _get_client()
    if not client:
        return None
    return client.nlp_parse(question)


def lightning_parse_amount(token: str) -> int | None:
    """
    Parse Indian currency token (10k, 5l, 2cr, etc.) to an integer.
    Tries the sidecar first; falls back to Python on failure.
    """
    client = _get_client()
    if client:
        return client.parse_amount(token)
    return _py_parse_amount(token)


def is_lookup_intent(parsed: dict) -> bool:
    """
    Return True when the NLP parsed result looks like a direct LOOKUP
    (single named entity, no aggregate operators) — should bypass SQL.
    """
    if not parsed:
        return False
    filters = parsed.get("Filters") or parsed.get("filters") or []
    text = (parsed.get("Text") or parsed.get("text") or "").lower()
    agg_words = {"sum", "total", "count", "average", "avg", "trend", "monthly"}
    if any(w in text.split() for w in agg_words):
        return False
    # If there are only equality / name filters it's a lookup
    if not filters:
        return bool(parsed.get("DocType") or parsed.get("doctype"))
    return True


# ── Python fallback for ParseAmount (mirrors rules.go) ────────────────────────

def _py_parse_amount(token: str) -> int | None:
    """Python replica of Go ParseAmount from nlp/rules.go."""
    if not token:
        return None
    token = token.lower().replace(",", "").strip()
    multipliers = {
        "k":       1_000,
        "l":       100_000,
        "lakh":    100_000,
        "lakhs":   100_000,
        "m":       1_000_000,
        "million": 1_000_000,
        "millions":1_000_000,
        "cr":      10_000_000,
        "crore":   10_000_000,
        "crores":  10_000_000,
    }
    # longest suffix first to avoid "cr" matching inside "crore"
    for suffix in sorted(multipliers, key=len, reverse=True):
        if token.endswith(suffix):
            prefix = token[: -len(suffix)].strip() or "1"
            try:
                return int(float(prefix) * multipliers[suffix])
            except ValueError:
                pass
    try:
        return int(float(token))
    except ValueError:
        return None


# ── Filters → SQL WHERE fragment ──────────────────────────────────────────────

def filters_to_where(filters: list) -> str:
    """
    Convert the Go NLP Filters list to a MySQL WHERE clause fragment.

    Each filter is a dict: {Field, Op, Value}
    This is used by 8.4 to pre-inject parsed filters into NeuralSQLBuilder.
    """
    clauses = []
    for f in filters:
        field = f.get("Field") or f.get("field", "")
        op    = f.get("Op") or f.get("op", "=")
        value = f.get("Value") or f.get("value")

        if not field or value is None:
            continue

        if op == "between" and isinstance(value, list) and len(value) == 2:
            # Date range: value is [start, end] strings
            start, end = value[0], value[1]
            # Strip time component if present
            if "T" in str(start):
                start = str(start)[:10]
            if "T" in str(end):
                end = str(end)[:10]
            clauses.append(f"`{field}` BETWEEN '{start}' AND '{end}'")
        elif op in (">", "<", ">=", "<=", "=", "!="):
            if isinstance(value, str):
                safe = value.replace("'", "''")
                clauses.append(f"`{field}` {op} '{safe}'")
            else:
                clauses.append(f"`{field}` {op} {value}")
        elif op == "=":
            safe = str(value).replace("'", "''")
            clauses.append(f"`{field}` = '{safe}'")

    return " AND ".join(clauses)

# FILE: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/f_genbi/f_genbi/f_genbi/genbi/local_engine.py
"""
GenBI Local Engine — No-LLM SQL Generation.

Chains the existing genbi/ NLP modules to generate SQL from natural language
without any external LLM API call.

Pipeline:
  Question
    → IntentClassifier  (INTENT)
    → EntityResolver    (DocTypes)
    → QueryParser       (fields, filters, group_by, aggregates)
    → schema_builder    (field names + table info)
    → SQLTemplateEngine (fills template → valid SQL)
    → same downstream execution + visualization as LLM path

Handles ~70% of everyday queries:
  LIST, AGGREGATE, TREND, LOOKUP, JOIN, COMPARE
Falls back with a helpful message for unsupported patterns.
"""

import re

import frappe
from frappe import _


# ── Intent → SQL strategy map ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

class SQLTemplateEngine:
	"""Builds safe, parameterised SQL from a parsed query structure."""

	# Useful columns to project for common DocTypes (no SELECT *)
	_DEFAULT_COLUMNS = {
		"Sales Invoice":   "`name`, `customer_name`, `posting_date`, `grand_total`, `outstanding_amount`, `status`",
		"Purchase Invoice":"`name`, `supplier_name`, `posting_date`, `grand_total`, `outstanding_amount`, `status`",
		"Sales Order":     "`name`, `customer_name`, `transaction_date`, `grand_total`, `status`",
		"Purchase Order":  "`name`, `supplier_name`, `transaction_date`, `grand_total`, `status`",
		"Customer":        "`name`, `customer_name`, `customer_group`, `territory`, `disabled`",
		"Supplier":        "`name`, `supplier_name`, `supplier_group`, `disabled`",
		"Item":            "`name`, `item_name`, `item_group`, `stock_uom`, `disabled`",
		"Employee":        "`name`, `employee_name`, `department`, `designation`, `status`",
		"Payment Entry":   "`name`, `party`, `payment_type`, `posting_date`, `paid_amount`",
		"Lead":            "`name`, `lead_name`, `company_name`, `status`, `lead_owner`",
		"Opportunity":     "`name`, `opportunity_from`, `party_name`, `status`, `expected_closing`",
	}

	_SUBMITTABLE = {
		"Sales Invoice", "Purchase Invoice", "Sales Order", "Purchase Order",
		"Delivery Note", "Purchase Receipt", "Stock Entry", "Journal Entry",
		"Payment Entry", "Salary Slip",
	}

	def __init__(self, intent: str, parse: dict, schema: dict, question: str = ""):
		self.intent = intent
		self.parse = parse
		self.schema = schema
		self.question = question.lower()

	# ── Public entry point ────────────────────────────────────────────────────

	def build(self) -> str | None:
		"""Return a safe SQL string or None if the pattern is unsupported."""
		dispatch = {
			"LIST":      self._build_list,
			"LOOKUP":    self._build_lookup,
			"AGGREGATE": self._build_aggregate,
			"TREND":     self._build_trend,
			"JOIN":      self._build_join,
			"COMPARE":   self._build_compare,
		}
		builder = dispatch.get(self.intent)
		return builder() if builder else None

	# ── Intent builders ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

	def _build_list(self) -> str | None:
		doctype = self._primary_doctype()
		if not doctype:
			return None

		columns = self._columns_for(doctype)
		where   = self._where_clause(doctype)
		limit   = self._detect_limit()

		return (
			f"SELECT {columns} FROM `tab{doctype}`"
			+ (f" WHERE {where}" if where else "")
			+ f" ORDER BY `creation` DESC LIMIT {limit}"
		)

	def _build_lookup(self) -> str | None:
		"""LIKE-search for a named entity in the question."""
		doctype = self._primary_doctype()
		if not doctype:
			return None

		# Find a search term: a quoted string or a capitalized word not matching a DocType
		search_term = self._extract_search_term()
		columns = self._columns_for(doctype)

		if search_term:
			name_field = self._label_field(doctype)
			return (
				f"SELECT {columns} FROM `tab{doctype}`"
				f" WHERE `name` LIKE '%{search_term}%'"
				f"  OR `{name_field}` LIKE '%{search_term}%'"
				f" LIMIT 20"
			)

		return self._build_list()  # fallback to plain list

	def _build_aggregate(self) -> str | None:
		doctype = self._primary_doctype()
		if not doctype:
			return None

		agg_fn, agg_field = self._detect_aggregate(doctype)
		group_field       = self._detect_group_by(doctype)
		where             = self._where_clause(doctype)
		limit             = self._detect_limit()

		if group_field:
			return (
				f"SELECT `{group_field}`, {agg_fn}(`{agg_field}`) AS `total`"
				f" FROM `tab{doctype}`"
				+ (f" WHERE {where}" if where else "")
				+ f" GROUP BY `{group_field}` ORDER BY `total` DESC LIMIT {limit}"
			)

		# No group_by — simple scalar aggregate
		return (
			f"SELECT {agg_fn}(`{agg_field}`) AS `total`"
			f" FROM `tab{doctype}`"
			+ (f" WHERE {where}" if where else "")
		)

	def _build_trend(self) -> str | None:
		doctype    = self._primary_doctype()
		if not doctype:
			return None

		date_field  = self._detect_date_field(doctype)
		value_field = self._detect_value_field(doctype)

		if not date_field or not value_field:
			return None

		where = self._where_clause(doctype, exclude_date=True)
		return (
			f"SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`{date_field}`, '%Y-%m') AS `month`,"
			f" SUM(`{value_field}`) AS `total`"
			f" FROM `tab{doctype}`"
			+ (f" WHERE {where}" if where else "")
			+ f" GROUP BY `month` ORDER BY `month` DESC LIMIT 24"
		)

	def _build_join(self) -> str | None:
		"""Parent + first child table join."""
		entities = self.parse.get("entities", [])
		if len(entities) < 2:
			return None

		parent, child = entities[0], entities[1]
		parent_cols = self._columns_for(parent, alias="p")
		child_cols  = ", ".join([
			"`c`.`item_code`", "`c`.`item_name`",
			"`c`.`qty`", "`c`.`rate`", "`c`.`amount`",
		])
		where = self._where_clause(parent, alias="p")

		return (
			f"SELECT {parent_cols}, {child_cols}"
			f" FROM `tab{parent}` p"
			f" JOIN `tab{child}` c ON c.`parent` = p.`name`"
			+ (f" WHERE {where}" if where else "")
			+ " LIMIT 100"
		)

	def _build_compare(self) -> str | None:
		"""Two time-period aggregate side-by-side (this vs last)."""
		doctype     = self._primary_doctype()
		if not doctype:
			return None

		date_field  = self._detect_date_field(doctype)
		value_field = self._detect_value_field(doctype)
		if not date_field or not value_field:
			return self._build_aggregate()  # fallback

		return (
			f"SELECT"
			f"  SUM(CASE WHEN YEAR(`{date_field}`) = YEAR(CURDATE())"
			f"           AND MONTH(`{date_field}`) = MONTH(CURDATE())"
			f"      THEN `{value_field}` ELSE 0 END) AS `this_month`,"
			f"  SUM(CASE WHEN `{date_field}` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL 12 MONTH)"
			f"      THEN `{value_field}` ELSE 0 END) AS `last_12_months`"
			f" FROM `tab{doctype}`"
		)

	# ── Helper: entity extraction ─────────────────────────────────────────────

	def _primary_doctype(self) -> str | None:
		entities = self.parse.get("entities", [])
		return entities[0] if entities else None

	def _columns_for(self, doctype: str, alias: str = "") -> str:
		defaults = self._DEFAULT_COLUMNS.get(doctype)
		if defaults:
			if alias:
				# Prefix each backtick column with alias
				return re.sub(r"`(\w[^`]*)`", lambda m: f"`{alias}`.`{m.group(1)}`", defaults)
			return defaults

		# Fallback: get in_list_view fields from meta
		try:
			meta = frappe.get_meta(doctype)
			cols = ["`name`"]
			for f in meta.fields:
				if getattr(f, "in_list_view", 0) and f.fieldname not in ("name",):
					cols.append(f"`{f.fieldname}`")
				if len(cols) >= 8:
					break
			result = ", ".join(cols)
			if alias:
				return re.sub(r"`(\w[^`]*)`", lambda m: f"`{alias}`.`{m.group(1)}`", result)
			return result
		except Exception:
			return f"`{alias}`.`name`" if alias else "`name`"

	def _label_field(self, doctype: str) -> str:
		"""Return the human-name field for a DocType (e.g. customer_name, item_name)."""
		guesses = [
			f"{doctype.lower().replace(' ', '_')}_name",
			"full_name", "title", "subject", "label",
		]
		try:
			meta = frappe.get_meta(doctype)
			existing = {f.fieldname for f in meta.fields}
			for g in guesses:
				if g in existing:
					return g
		except Exception:
			pass
		return "name"

	# ── Helper: filter / date detection ──────────────────────────────────────

	def _where_clause(self, doctype: str, alias: str = "", exclude_date: bool = False) -> str:
		parts = []
		prefix = f"`{alias}`." if alias else ""

		# docstatus filter for submittable doctypes
		if doctype in self._SUBMITTABLE:
			parts.append(f"{prefix}`docstatus` = 1")

		filters = self.parse.get("filters", [])
		for f in filters:
			if f.get("doctype", doctype) != doctype:
				continue
			field = f.get("field", "")
			op    = f.get("op", "=")
			val   = f.get("value", "")
			if field and val is not None:
				parts.append(f"{prefix}`{field}` {op} '{val}'")

		if not exclude_date:
			date_filter = self._detect_date_filter(doctype)
			if date_filter:
				parts.append(date_filter.replace("`", prefix + "`") if alias else date_filter)

		return " AND ".join(parts) if parts else ""

	def _detect_date_filter(self, doctype: str) -> str:
		q = self.question
		date_field = self._detect_date_field(doctype)
		if not date_field:
			return ""

		if re.search(r"today", q):
			return f"`{date_field}` = CURDATE()"
		if re.search(r"this\s+month|current\s+month", q):
			return f"`{date_field}` BETWEEN DATE_FORMAT(NOW(), '%Y-%m-01') AND LAST_DAY(NOW())"
		if re.search(r"this\s+year|current\s+year", q):
			return f"YEAR(`{date_field}`) = YEAR(CURDATE())"
		if re.search(r"last\s+month", q):
			return (
				f"`{date_field}` BETWEEN DATE_FORMAT(DATE_SUB(NOW(), INTERVAL 1 MONTH), '%Y-%m-01')"
				f" AND LAST_DAY(DATE_SUB(NOW(), INTERVAL 1 MONTH))"
			)
		if re.search(r"last\s+7\s+days|past\s+week", q):
			return f"`{date_field}` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL 7 DAY)"
		if re.search(r"last\s+30\s+days", q):
			return f"`{date_field}` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(), INTERVAL 30 DAY)"
		if re.search(r"last\s+year", q):
			return f"YEAR(`{date_field}`) = YEAR(CURDATE()) - 1"
		return ""

	def _detect_date_field(self, doctype: str) -> str:
		candidates = ["posting_date", "transaction_date", "date", "creation"]
		try:
			meta = frappe.get_meta(doctype)
			existing = {f.fieldname for f in meta.fields if f.fieldtype in ("Date", "Datetime")}
			for c in candidates:
				if c in existing:
					return c
			return next(iter(existing), "")
		except Exception:
			return ""

	def _detect_value_field(self, doctype: str) -> str:
		candidates = ["grand_total", "total", "amount", "net_total", "paid_amount", "base_grand_total"]
		try:
			meta = frappe.get_meta(doctype)
			existing = {f.fieldname for f in meta.fields if f.fieldtype in ("Currency", "Float")}
			for c in candidates:
				if c in existing:
					return c
			return next(iter(existing), "")
		except Exception:
			return ""

	def _detect_aggregate(self, doctype: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
		q = self.question
		value_field = self._detect_value_field(doctype)
		count_field = "name"

		if re.search(r"\bcount\b|\bhow many\b|\bnumber of\b", q):
			return ("COUNT", count_field)
		if re.search(r"\baverage\b|\bavg\b", q):
			return ("AVG", value_field or count_field)
		# Default: SUM
		return ("SUM", value_field or count_field)

	def _detect_group_by(self, doctype: str) -> str:
		"""Detect a likely group-by field from question keywords."""
		q = self.question
		group_candidates = {
			r"by customer|per customer":    "customer_name",
			r"by supplier|per supplier":    "supplier_name",
			r"by item|per item":            "item_code",
			r"by month|monthly|per month":  "DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`, '%Y-%m')",
			r"by year|yearly|per year":     "YEAR(`posting_date`)",
			r"by department":               "department",
			r"by territory|per territory":  "territory",
			r"by status":                   "status",
			r"by warehouse":                "warehouse",
			r"by company":                  "company",
		}
		for pattern, field in group_candidates.items():
			if re.search(pattern, q):
				return field

		parse_group = self.parse.get("group_by", {})
		return parse_group.get("field", "")

	def _detect_limit(self) -> int:
		q = self.question
		m = re.search(r"\btop\s+(\d+)\b|\blast\s+(\d+)\b|\b(\d+)\s+(?:rows?|records?|results?)\b", q)
		if m:
			return int(next(g for g in m.groups() if g is not None))
		return 50

	def _extract_search_term(self) -> str:
		# Quoted string: "Acme Corp"
		m = re.search(r'"([^"]+)"|\'([^\']+)\'', self.question)
		if m:
			return m.group(1) or m.group(2)
		# Pattern: "details of X", "about X", "find X"
		m = re.search(r"(?:details of|about|find|search for|info on|info about|contact of)\s+([A-Z][\w\s]{1,30})", self.question, re.I)
		if m:
			return m.group(1).strip()
		return ""


# ── Pattern-based fast path ───────────────────────────────────────────────────

class PatternEngine:
	"""
	Regex-based SQL generator for the most common ERPNext BI queries.

	Runs BEFORE the NLP pipeline.  Each pattern maps a plain-English question
	shape to a correct, parameterised SQL string.  All SQL uses only
	docstatus-filtered, safe literals — no user-supplied values are injected.

	Patterns are tried in order; first match wins.
	"""

	# ── helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

	@staticmethod
	def _limit(q: str, default: int = 10) -> int:
		m = re.search(r"\btop\s+(\d+)\b|\bfirst\s+(\d+)\b|\b(\d+)\s+(?:rows?|records?|results?)\b", q)
		if m:
			return int(next(g for g in m.groups() if g is not None))
		return default

	@staticmethod
	def _date_filter(q: str, field: str) -> str:
		"""Return a WHERE fragment for the most common relative-date keywords."""
		if re.search(r"\btoday\b", q):
			return f"`{field}` = CURDATE()"
		if re.search(r"\bthis\s+month\b|\bcurrent\s+month\b", q):
			return f"`{field}` BETWEEN DATE_FORMAT(NOW(),'%Y-%m-01') AND LAST_DAY(NOW())"
		if re.search(r"\blast\s+month\b", q):
			return (
				f"`{field}` BETWEEN DATE_FORMAT(DATE_SUB(NOW(),INTERVAL 1 MONTH),'%Y-%m-01')"
				f" AND LAST_DAY(DATE_SUB(NOW(),INTERVAL 1 MONTH))"
			)
		if re.search(r"\bthis\s+year\b|\bcurrent\s+year\b", q):
			return f"YEAR(`{field}`) = YEAR(CURDATE())"
		if re.search(r"\blast\s+year\b", q):
			return f"YEAR(`{field}`) = YEAR(CURDATE()) - 1"
		if re.search(r"\blast\s+30\s+days\b", q):
			return f"`{field}` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(),INTERVAL 30 DAY)"
		if re.search(r"\blast\s+7\s+days\b|\bpast\s+week\b", q):
			return f"`{field}` >= DATE_SUB(CURDATE(),INTERVAL 7 DAY)"
		return ""

	# ── pattern library ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

	# ── name extractor ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

	@staticmethod
	def _extract_name(question: str, strip_words: list[str]) -> str:
		"""
		Extract a proper-noun name from the question.

		1. Try quoted string first: 'Grant Plastics Ltd' or "Acme Corp"
		2. Strip leading noise words (e.g. "the customer", "about the supplier")
		   then take the remainder up to a sentence-ending punctuation.
		3. Fall back to the longest capitalised word-run in the original question.
		"""
		# Quoted string — highest confidence
		m = re.search(r'"([^"]{2,60})"|\'([^\']{2,60})\'', question)
		if m:
			return (m.group(1) or m.group(2)).strip()

		# Build a pattern that strips noise prefixes, then capture the name
		# e.g. "some details of the customer Grant Plastics Ltd."
		#       → strip "some details of the customer " → "Grant Plastics Ltd."
		noise_re = (
			r"(?:(?:show|give|get|find|fetch|tell\s+me|what\s+(?:is|are))\s+)?"
			r"(?:(?:some\s+)?(?:details?|info(?:rmation)?|data|record|profile)\s+(?:of|about|for|on)\s+)?"
			r"(?:(?:about|for|of|on)\s+)?"
			r"(?:the\s+)?"
			r"(?:" + "|".join(re.escape(w) for w in strip_words) + r")\s+"
			r"(?:the\s+|of\s+)?"
		)
		m = re.search(noise_re + r"(.+?)[\.\?!]*$", question, re.I)
		if m:
			return m.group(m.lastindex).strip().rstrip(".")

		# Longest run of words starting with a capital letter
		m = re.search(r"([A-Z][A-Za-z0-9&.,'\- ]{2,50})", question)
		if m:
			return m.group(1).strip()

		return ""

	def match(self, question: str) -> dict | None:
		"""Return a result dict or None if no pattern matches."""
		q = question.lower().strip()

		# ── Lookup: single entity by name ─────────────────────────────────────
		# These run first — a question mentioning a specific name should not fall
		# through to aggregates.

		# Customer lookup: "details of [name]", "about customer [name]", etc.
		if re.search(
			r"\bcustomer\b.{0,60}(?:[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9&.,'\- ]{2,})"   # "customer X"
			r"|(?:details?|info|profile|about|find|show|get)\b.{0,40}\bcustomer",  # "details of customer"
			question
		):
			name = self._extract_name(question, ["customer", "client"])
			if name:
				sql = (
					f"SELECT `name`, `customer_name`, `customer_type`, `customer_group`,"
					f" `territory`, `mobile_no`, `email_id`, `disabled`"
					f" FROM `tabCustomer`"
					f" WHERE `name` LIKE '%{name}%'"
					f"    OR `customer_name` LIKE '%{name}%'"
					f" LIMIT 10"
				)
				return self._result(question, sql, "table", f"Customer: {name}")

		# Supplier lookup
		if re.search(
			r"\bsupplier\b.{0,60}(?:[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9&.,'\- ]{2,})"
			r"|(?:details?|info|profile|about|find|show|get)\b.{0,40}\bsupplier",
			question
		):
			name = self._extract_name(question, ["supplier", "vendor"])
			if name:
				sql = (
					f"SELECT `name`, `supplier_name`, `supplier_type`, `supplier_group`,"
					f" `country`, `mobile_no`, `email_id`, `disabled`"
					f" FROM `tabSupplier`"
					f" WHERE `name` LIKE '%{name}%'"
					f"    OR `supplier_name` LIKE '%{name}%'"
					f" LIMIT 10"
				)
				return self._result(question, sql, "table", f"Supplier: {name}")

		# Item lookup
		if re.search(
			r"\bitem\b.{0,60}(?:[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9&.,'\- ]{2,})"
			r"|(?:details?|info|profile|about|find|show|get)\b.{0,40}\bitem",
			question
		):
			name = self._extract_name(question, ["item", "product", "sku"])
			if name:
				sql = (
					f"SELECT `name`, `item_name`, `item_group`, `stock_uom`,"
					f" `description`, `disabled`"
					f" FROM `tabItem`"
					f" WHERE `name` LIKE '%{name}%'"
					f"    OR `item_name` LIKE '%{name}%'"
					f" LIMIT 10"
				)
				return self._result(question, sql, "table", f"Item: {name}")

		# Generic lookup — quoted name + no aggregate keywords
		if not re.search(r"\btop\b|\btotal\b|\bcount\b|\bsum\b|\baverage\b|\btrend\b|\bmonthly\b", q):
			quoted = re.search(r'"([^"]{2,60})"|\'([^\']{2,60})\'', question)
			if quoted:
				name = (quoted.group(1) or quoted.group(2)).strip()
				# Try to infer doctype from question keywords
				dt_map = {
					r"\bcustomer": ("Customer", "`name`, `customer_name`, `customer_group`, `territory`, `email_id`"),
					r"\bsupplier": ("Supplier", "`name`, `supplier_name`, `supplier_group`, `country`, `email_id`"),
					r"\bitem|\bproduct": ("Item", "`name`, `item_name`, `item_group`, `stock_uom`"),
					r"\bemployee": ("Employee", "`name`, `employee_name`, `department`, `designation`, `status`"),
					r"\blead": ("Lead", "`name`, `lead_name`, `company_name`, `status`, `email_id`"),
				}
				for pat, (doctype, cols) in dt_map.items():
					if re.search(pat, q):
						sql = (
							f"SELECT {cols} FROM `tab{doctype}`"
							f" WHERE `name` LIKE '%{name}%' LIMIT 10"
						)
						return self._result(question, sql, "table", f"{doctype}: {name}")

		# ── Sales / Revenue ──────────────────────────────────────────────────

		# top N customers by revenue/sales
		if re.search(r"\btop\b.*\bcustomer.*\b(revenue|sales|amount|total|grand total|billing|invoic)\b"
					 r"|\bcustomer.*\btop\b.*\b(revenue|sales|amount|total)\b"
					 r"|\bbest\s+customer|\blargest\s+customer", q):
			lim = self._limit(q)
			df = self._date_filter(q, "posting_date")
			where = f"docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT `customer_name`, SUM(`grand_total`) AS `total_revenue`,"
				f" COUNT(`name`) AS `invoice_count`"
				f" FROM `tabSales Invoice`"
				f" WHERE {where}"
				f" GROUP BY `customer_name`"
				f" ORDER BY `total_revenue` DESC LIMIT {lim}"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "bar", f"Top {lim} customers by revenue")

		# top N items by sales quantity or amount
		if re.search(r"\btop\b.*\bitem.*\b(sales?|revenue|amount|sold|quantity|qty)\b"
					 r"|\bitem.*\btop\b.*\b(sales?|revenue|sold)\b"
					 r"|\bbest.sell\w*\s+item|\bmost\s+sold\s+item", q):
			lim = self._limit(q)
			df = self._date_filter(q, "si.posting_date")
			where = f"si.docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			if re.search(r"\bquantity\b|\bqty\b|\bunits?\b", q):
				agg = "SUM(`sii`.`qty`) AS `total_qty`"
				order_col = "total_qty"
			else:
				agg = "SUM(`sii`.`amount`) AS `total_sales`"
				order_col = "total_sales"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT `sii`.`item_code`, `sii`.`item_name`, {agg}"
				f" FROM `tabSales Invoice Item` sii"
				f" JOIN `tabSales Invoice` si ON si.`name` = sii.`parent`"
				f" WHERE {where}"
				f" GROUP BY `sii`.`item_code`, `sii`.`item_name`"
				f" ORDER BY `{order_col}` DESC LIMIT {lim}"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "bar", f"Top {lim} items by sales")

		# total revenue / total sales (scalar or time-grouped)
		if re.search(r"\btotal\s+(revenue|sales|billing|invoiced|grand total)\b"
					 r"|\brevenue\s+total\b|\bsales\s+total\b"
					 r"|\bhow\s+much\s+(revenue|sales|did we make|have we made)\b", q):
			df = self._date_filter(q, "posting_date")
			where = f"docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT SUM(`grand_total`) AS `total_revenue`,"
				f" COUNT(`name`) AS `invoice_count`"
				f" FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE {where}"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "number", "Total revenue")

		# monthly revenue / sales trend
		if re.search(r"\b(monthly|by\s+month|per\s+month|month.wise|month\s+over\s+month)\b.*\b(sales?|revenue|invoic)\b"
					 r"|\b(sales?|revenue|invoic)\b.*\b(monthly|by\s+month|trend|over\s+time|per\s+month)\b", q):
			df = self._date_filter(q, "posting_date")
			where = f"docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`,'%Y-%m') AS `month`,"
				f" SUM(`grand_total`) AS `total_revenue`,"
				f" COUNT(`name`) AS `invoice_count`"
				f" FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE {where}"
				f" GROUP BY `month` ORDER BY `month` DESC LIMIT 24"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "line", "Monthly revenue trend")

		# sales by territory
		if re.search(r"\bsales?\s+by\s+territory\b|\brevenue\s+by\s+territory\b|\bterritory.wise\s+sales?\b", q):
			df = self._date_filter(q, "posting_date")
			where = f"docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT `territory`, SUM(`grand_total`) AS `total_revenue`"
				f" FROM `tabSales Invoice` WHERE {where}"
				f" GROUP BY `territory` ORDER BY `total_revenue` DESC LIMIT 20"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "bar", "Sales by territory")

		# pending / outstanding sales invoices
		if re.search(r"\b(pending|outstanding|unpaid|overdue)\b.*\b(invoice|invoic|bill)\b"
					 r"|\binvoice\b.*\b(pending|outstanding|unpaid|overdue)\b", q):
			lim = self._limit(q, 50)
			sql = (
				f"SELECT `name`, `customer_name`, `posting_date`, `due_date`,"
				f" `grand_total`, `outstanding_amount`, `status`"
				f" FROM `tabSales Invoice`"
				f" WHERE `docstatus` = 1 AND `outstanding_amount` > 0"
				f" ORDER BY `outstanding_amount` DESC LIMIT {lim}"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "table", "Outstanding invoices")

		# ── Purchase / Supplier ──────────────────────────────────────────────

		# top N suppliers by purchase amount
		if re.search(r"\btop\b.*\bsupplier.*\b(purchase|amount|total|spend|buying)\b"
					 r"|\bsupplier.*\btop\b.*\b(purchase|spend|amount)\b"
					 r"|\bbiggest\s+supplier|\blargest\s+supplier", q):
			lim = self._limit(q)
			df = self._date_filter(q, "posting_date")
			where = f"docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT `supplier_name`, SUM(`grand_total`) AS `total_purchase`,"
				f" COUNT(`name`) AS `bill_count`"
				f" FROM `tabPurchase Invoice`"
				f" WHERE {where}"
				f" GROUP BY `supplier_name`"
				f" ORDER BY `total_purchase` DESC LIMIT {lim}"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "bar", f"Top {lim} suppliers by purchase")

		# monthly purchase trend
		if re.search(r"\b(monthly|by\s+month|per\s+month|month.wise)\b.*\b(purchase|procurement|buying)\b"
					 r"|\b(purchase|procurement)\b.*\b(monthly|by\s+month|trend|over\s+time)\b", q):
			df = self._date_filter(q, "posting_date")
			where = f"docstatus = 1{' AND ' + df if df else ''}"
			sql = (
				f"SELECT DATE_FORMAT(`posting_date`,'%Y-%m') AS `month`,"
				f" SUM(`grand_total`) AS `total_purchase`"
				f" FROM `tabPurchase Invoice` WHERE {where}"
				f" GROUP BY `month` ORDER BY `month` DESC LIMIT 24"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "line", "Monthly purchase trend")

		# pending purchase invoices
		if re.search(r"\b(pending|outstanding|unpaid)\b.*\b(purchase\s+invoice|purchase\s+bill)\b"
					 r"|\bpurchase\s+(invoice|bill)\b.*\b(pending|outstanding|unpaid)\b", q):
			lim = self._limit(q, 50)
			sql = (
				f"SELECT `name`, `supplier_name`, `posting_date`, `due_date`,"
				f" `grand_total`, `outstanding_amount`, `status`"
				f" FROM `tabPurchase Invoice`"
				f" WHERE `docstatus` = 1 AND `outstanding_amount` > 0"
				f" ORDER BY `outstanding_amount` DESC LIMIT {lim}"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "table", "Pending purchase invoices")

		# ── Inventory ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

		# low stock / items below reorder level
		if re.search(r"\blow\s+stock\b|\bbelow\s+reorder\b|\breorder\s+level\b|\bstock\s+alert\b"
					 r"|\bitem\b.*\bstock\s+out\b|\bout\s+of\s+stock\b", q):
			sql = (
				"SELECT b.`item_code`, b.`warehouse`,"
				" b.`actual_qty`, b.`reserved_qty`,"
				" b.`actual_qty` - b.`reserved_qty` AS `available_qty`,"
				" i.`reorder_level`"
				" FROM `tabBin` b"
				" JOIN `tabItem` i ON i.`name` = b.`item_code`"
				" WHERE b.`actual_qty` <= i.`reorder_level` AND i.`disabled` = 0"
				" ORDER BY `available_qty` ASC LIMIT 50"
			)
			return self._result(question, sql, "table", "Items at or below reorder level")

		# top N items by current stock value
		if re.search(r"\btop\b.*\bitem.*\b(stock|inventory|warehouse)\b"
					 r"|\bitem.*\b(highest|most)\b.*\bstock\b"
					 r"|\bstock\s+value\b|\binventory\s+value\b", q):
			lim = self._limit(q)
